There is no shot map, xG timeline, pass network, heat map, average positions, or per-player statistic, and no player rating exists. Source: web/stats.mjs:8; web/report.mjs:10; crates/engine/src/sim.rs:384.
- Nothing ranks moments by importance and there is no highlight level: the feed marks goals, the skip button jumps to the next stoppage, and the report lists goals and cards. Source: web/feed.mjs:158; web/main.mjs:871; web/report.mjs:43.
- The commentator remembers only past event kinds, goal times per club, and who is booked, so it cannot say that a player has scored in three matches running. Source: crates/engine/src/commentary/context.rs:15.
- Commentary today is text from templates only: 54 template sets and 168 lines, at least three plain lines per commented kind. A set can require the minute band, the score state, a scoring streak, a booked player, and a repeat of the same kind; a line used in the last ten minutes is skipped. Shots and passes get no line, and no audio exists. Source: content/commentary/en.json; crates/engine/src/commentary/templates.rs:19; crates/engine/src/commentary/mod.rs:104.

### Player decision-making and off-ball movement

- The research catalogue lists 53 actions seen in real matches, 38 outfield and 15 goalkeeper, 39 measured in full, 10 in part, feints thinly, and three not at all: marking and cover assignments, keeper organisation, and keeper dive kinematics. A match holds about 1,000 possessions, 2,415 passing options offered, 495 runs, 292 pressures, and about 280 carries of 5 metres or more. Source: .scratch/out/ACTIONS.md §1 and §15.
- The published pitch-control model, which says who reaches each point first, uses a 0.7-second reaction, 5 metres a second top speed, an arrival-time spread of 0.45 seconds, and a ball speed of 15 metres a second. Source: https://github.com/Friends-of-Tracking-Data-FoTD/LaurieOnTracking.
- Passes allowed per defensive action (PPDA) average 9.9 to 11.8 across the top five leagues (2019-20), and Premier League teams span about 8.8 to 17.7. Source: https://totalfootballanalysis.com/data-analysis/data-analysis-ppda-its-definition-advantages-and-disadvantages; https://www.premierleague.com/en/news/4250153/passes-per-defensive-action-explained.
- The same defender is still the nearest to an attacker after 1 second 88.5 percent of the time, after 5 seconds 64.0, and after 10 seconds 53.5, and in the box 80, 51, and 38: defenders hand runners over rather than follow them. Defenders stand goal-side 63 to 73 percent of the time. Source: .scratch/out/gap_idsse_an.txt (DFL 7 matches).
- The RoboCup simulator limits perception with a 90-degree view that a player can halve or double, a wider view refreshing less often, players within 3 metres sensed outside the view, and identities certain only to 20 metres. Source: https://rcsoccersim.readthedocs.io/en/latest/soccerserver.html.
- A fitted choice model picks the real pass target 46.9 percent of the time against 36 by chance, weighting the chance of completion, a receiver who is running, and an option ahead; threat barely counts, and players pick the most threatening option only 31.7 percent of the time. Source: .scratch/out/gap_sc.txt (conditional logit on SkillCorner passing options).
- Players scan about 0.44 times a second before receiving, central midfielders most and forwards least, elite players up to 0.6 to 0.8. Scanning falls under close pressure (0.38 against 0.48 a second) and to near zero near the opponent's goal. More scanning goes with slightly higher completion and more long forward passes, a small effect next to ability. Source: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7573254/ (Jordet and others 2020, 27 Premier League players, 9,574 possessions).
- A player keeps the ball for a median of about 1.2 seconds, with a 90th percentile of 3.3 to 3.7. With no pressure at receiving, 1.0 percent of receptions are lost, 5.6 percent are played first time, and 93.4 percent of passes complete; under very high pressure the figures are 32.6, 31.3, and 67.0 percent. Forwards hold the ball about 0.9 seconds per contact and keepers about 2.1. Source: .scratch/out/gap_sc.txt; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5503225/ (Bundesliga 2012-13, 69,667 ball intervals).
- The 495 off-ball runs a match split into: ahead of the ball 135.8, support 82.6, coming short 63.7, dropping off 54.8, cross receiver 46.8, in behind 37.4, pulling wide 32.2, overlap 15.0, pulling into the half-space 14.7, and underlap 11.6. Forwards make about 98 runs a match and wingers about 189. No public source counts third-man runs. Source: .scratch/out/ACTIONS.md §8.1 (SkillCorner 20 matches).
- Players rarely crowd the ball: of 20 outfielders, 1.62 are within 5 metres of the ball, 3.71 within 10, and 6.24 within 15; numbers near the ball are even between the teams; in 51.5 percent of live frames at most one player is within 5 metres; and 81 percent of outfielders are more than 10 metres away. Source: .scratch/out/FINDINGS.md §13.3 (DFL, about 154,000 live frames).
- The defending team has 2.6 players goal-side of the ball when the ball is within 20 metres of its goal, rising to 9.3 when the ball is 88 metres or more away; 0.5 to 0.7 attackers stand beyond the last line. Source: .scratch/out/FINDINGS.md §12 (DFL 7 matches).
- Each defending line sits at a set distance from its own goal that grows with the ball's distance d from that goal: the last line at 5.9 + 0.486 d metres, the midfield line at 15.6 + 0.611 d, and the front line at 30.1 + 0.591 d. The back-to-midfield gap is 8 to 18 metres and the midfield-to-front gap 12 to 16. Source: .scratch/out/FINDINGS.md §12 (DFL 7 matches).
- Team width is about 45 to 46 metres in possession and 35 to 37 out of it; across 52 team-halves the ranges are 39.0 to 52.8 and 32.4 to 39.7, so the phase gap of about 10 metres is a hard band. Depth is 34 to 36 metres in possession and 30 to 33 out of it, and width barely changes with the ball's side. Source: .scratch/out/FINDINGS.md §12 (SkillCorner 20 matches; DFL 7 matches, 52 team-halves).
- The ball carrier scores every team-mate between 4 and 45 metres whose passing lane is clear enough; nothing limits what the carrier notices. Vision only adds a bonus to forward passes, and one noise term from decisions and composure blurs every score. Source: crates/engine/src/decision.rs:116; crates/engine/src/decision.rs:166.
- Pressure is one on-off flag, an opponent within 2.5 metres. The carrier re-decides on every tick with no time to decide, and holding the ball only loses a little score each second. Source: crates/engine/src/decision.rs:138; crates/engine/src/decision.rs:216.
- A pass aims at the team-mate's current position with an error from passing skill; there is no lead pass into space and no through ball onto a run. Source: crates/engine/src/decision.rs:272.
- Every player without the ball who is not pressing moves to a formation anchor: the formation slot, moved by block depth and duty, widened by the plan, and shifted with the ball by two fixed gains. The anchor is the same in and out of possession, and nobody makes a run, an overlap, a support angle, or a third-man move. Source: crates/engine/src/team.rs:258; crates/engine/src/decision.rs:42.
- Out of possession, the nearest opponents within the pressing distance, as many as the instruction allows up to four, run straight at the ball and the rest hold their anchors. There is no cover, no tracking of runners, no marking, and the line is only the anchors. A loose ball draws the nearest player of each team. Source: crates/engine/src/decision.rs:31; crates/engine/src/decision.rs:65.
- A dribble targets a point 8 metres toward goal and 4 metres away from the nearest opponent; there is no path choice and no move such as a turn, a feint, or cutting inside. Source: crates/engine/src/decision.rs:299.
- Habits of play are decided on the board but nothing in the engine reads them; the only per-player difference in choices is the role's small offsets to shooting, progress, and dribbling. Source: crates/engine/src/decision.rs:158; crates/engine/src/decision.rs:191.

### Cohesion across areas

- Whether 50 or 100 matches is enough depends on how often the thing happens. With 100 matches, mean goals has a standard error of about 0.17, so only a shift of about a third of a goal shows; the goalless share (6.3 percent) has an error of about 2.4 points and the red-card share about 3.7, so those bands cannot be judged at all. Statistics with many events per match, such as passes and pass completion, are judged well at 100. A one-attribute change on one player moves team goals by a few hundredths, so it needs tens of thousands of unpaired matches. Source: Poisson and binomial standard errors on the session-7 targets (2.83 goals, 6.3 percent goalless, 16.8 percent with a red card).
- Paired seeds only remove noise if one change does not reshuffle every later random draw. In a tick engine, one different draw changes the whole rest of the match unless each player and each kind of action draws from its own random stream. The engine replays from one seed today, so paired seeds need split streams first. Source: crates/engine/tests/determinism.rs:1.
- The checks can run on a Mac mini and a VPS. Because a match replays identically on every supported machine, one batch can be split across both and the reports still agree. Source: the person's answer in session 7.
- The checks chosen in session seven cost far more than the harness plans for. One match costs about 0.43 seconds of processor time on the reference desktop today. The eight-style round-robin needs about 39,000 matches (about 35 minutes on 8 cores), the sensitivity family about 830,000 (about 12 hours), and a full release run about 1.5 million (about 22 hours), before the realism work makes each match slower. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/05c-benchmark.md:128.
- The player's matchday is light beside the harness: three active leagues of ten matches cost about 13 processor-seconds today, about 3 seconds on a 4-core laptop, so even a several-fold slower engine keeps a matchday wait short. The heavy load of session seven falls on the checks, not on the player's machine. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/05c-benchmark.md:128.
- The three design documents predate session seven and disagree with it in 18 places. The largest: style from three layers where identity now acts off the pitch; personality traits on 1 to 100 where every rating is now 1 to 20 in tenths; DFL definitions for every band where passes and errors now follow Opta; 2017-18 Wyscout targets where targets are now five recent seasons per league and tier; an unchecked fast model; season bands that pass on real values; 1000 calibration matches; a noisy view of the manager's own squad; consistency as a visible attribute; and ball-local thinking where every player now thinks every tick. The later decision wins in each case, and every piece of work is marked stale. Source: docs/design/realism/01-engine-realism.md; docs/design/realism/02-event-contract-redesign.md; docs/design/realism/03-season-layer.md.
- Session seven depends on four things no document designs: how the ten position ratings map to about 40 roles; how saves and engine versions are numbered so that a mid-match save finishes on its old engine; event kinds for named errors, lapses, and video reviews, and body part, shot type, and pressure on every shot; and the storage of the effective-ability layer, relationships, and every pair. Source: docs/design/realism/01-engine-realism.md:337; docs/design/realism/02-event-contract-redesign.md:420.

### Match engine structure

- The pitch is fixed at 105 by 68 metres as constants; no ground can differ in size. Source: crates/engine/src/pitch.rs:7.
- Added time is already built from events: the rule pack prices each kind of stoppage and each card in seconds, a small random variance is added, and the total is clamped between a floor and a cap. The video referee does not exist yet, so its checks add nothing. Source: crates/engine/src/rules/clock.rs:28; crates/engine/src/rules/clock.rs:44.
- The same seed and build replay byte-identical tick files on the same machine; the test does not cover another machine or another operating system. Source: crates/engine/tests/determinism.rs:1.
- Dead-ball phases exist as stoppage kinds (kick-off, throw-in, corner, goal kick, penalty, free kick, and a drop ball after an injury), each with a delay and a chosen taker; no written state machine names the entry and exit of every phase. Source: crates/engine/src/rules/restart.rs:56.

### Tactical counterplay and team-level AI

- The calibration run already plays every pairing of formations against each other (up to 136 pairings), but it judges each pairing only for realism; it never checks whether one formation beats all the others. Source: crates/engine-cli/src/calibrate/mod.rs:399; crates/engine-cli/src/report/compare.rs:29.
- Each team instruction today is a number that scales one behaviour, for example a high line moves the block up by 5 metres; no instruction carries a designed cost, and nothing reads the squad's fit to it. Source: content/tactics.json:76.

### Realism calibration and validation

- Across 2021-22 to 2025-26 the top five leagues average, per match for both teams: 2.83 goals, 25.2 shots including blocked shots, 8.8 on target, 23.8 fouls, 9.7 corners, 4.08 yellow cards, a red card in 16.8 percent of matches, 0-0 in 6.3 percent, a 43.6, 25.3, and 31.1 percent home, draw, and away split, and 82.0 percent pass completion on Opta's count. Leagues differ: LaLiga shows reds in 22 percent of matches, the Premier League in 11. Source: https://www.football-data.co.uk/data.php; https://www.fotmob.com/leagues/47/stats/season/27110/teams/accurate_pass_team/premier-league-teams.
- The repository already has a calibration run: 1000 matches per sample over 17 bands, among them goals per match 2.4 to 3.2, goalless share 4 to 12 percent, shots on target share, corners, yellow cards, sending-off share, pass accuracy 75 to 88 percent, and goals per xG. The bands are still the wide first guesses, not the measured ones the board chose. Source: content/realism-bands.json:1; crates/engine-cli/src/calibrate/mod.rs:39.
- The calibration report compares two arms of one run (a feature flag off and on); it keeps no history across engine versions, and no automated job reruns it after each change. Source: crates/engine-cli/src/report/compare.rs:1.

### Action resolution and the probability layer

- Opta counts an error as one on-the-ball mistake, or a keeper's spill or failed claim, that directly leads to a shot or a goal. In the Premier League, errors led to 91, 119, and 167 goals in 2022-23 to 2024-25: about 0.24 to 0.44 a match, or 8 to 15 percent of all goals. Teams range from 1 to 18 a season, and keepers make about a quarter of them, about 0.1 a match. Source: https://theanalyst.com/articles/opta-football-stats-definitions; https://www.statmuse.com/fc/ask/premier-league-team-errors-leading-to-goals-2024-25.
- A shot today is a physical kick: the shooter aims at a spot whose width grows with finishing, noise shrinks with finishing, and the goal comes from the ball's flight and the keeper's reach. The keeper holds a fast ball with one flat chance of 86 percent, with no keeper attribute in it. Source: crates/engine/src/decision.rs:381; crates/engine/src/sim.rs:972; content/tuning.json:23.
- The engine computes an xG for each shot from distance and angle alone, and uses it only for the match report; it never decides an outcome. Source: crates/engine/src/sim.rs:440; crates/engine/src/sim.rs:877.
- A tackle today is a small chance on each tick for each opponent within reach, 5 percent times tackling divided by tackling plus dribbling. It reads no acceleration, agility, positioning, anticipation, or angle of approach. Source: crates/engine/src/sim.rs:992.

### The data model as an engine contract

- Football Manager shows attributes on 1 to 20 and keeps a hidden current ability on 1 to 200. An attribute point costs more of that ability budget above about 17, so top points are scarce because they are expensive, not because the display is curved. Source: https://www.fmscout.com/a-guide-to-current-ability-in-football-manager.html?pg=3.
- Football Manager is reported to move attributes in hidden fractions behind the whole-number display. A young player gains at most about one visible point per attribute per season, and two to three visible points across all attributes; the best tested season was about 11 ability points at age 21 against about 1 at age 28. Source: https://fm-arena.com/thread/2671-player-development-and-training/.
- Across 18,207 FIFA 19 players the overall rating is close to normal (mean 66.2, spread 6.9): 3 percent sit at 80 or above, 0.6 percent at 85 or above, and 14 players at 90 or above. Single attributes spread wider and lean left because of specialists. Market value grows about 1.21 times per rating point. Source: https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/javagarm/fifa-19-complete-player-dataset.
- Club Elo puts the English tiers at about 1839, 1608, 1491, and 1387, so the largest drop is from the first tier to the second (about a 79 percent win expectancy) and the drops shrink further down; elite clubs sit near 2030 to 2046. Source: http://clubelo.com/ENG.
- The world holds about 128,700 professional male players at about 3,990 clubs. Source: https://inside.fifa.com/legal/news/fifa-publishes-professional-football-report-2023.
- No published study shows that underlying skill is heavy-tailed. The honest reading is that skill is close to normal, while what depends on it (value, wages, goals) is convex and right-skewed, as superstar economics predicts; player value models show a thin elite over a dense middle. Source: https://milescorak.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/09/rosen-1981-aer-economics-superstars.pdf; https://tomdecroos.github.io/reports/xt_vs_vaep.pdf.
- A contest decided by the gap between two ratings on a logistic curve, as chess Elo does, gives 20 against 18 the same odds as 12 against 10. To make the top gap matter more on the pitch, each rating must first pass a convex map, for example an exponential, before the gap is taken. Baseball's 20-to-80 scouting scale sets 10 points to one standard deviation and stops at three. Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley%E2%80%93Terry_model; https://blogs.fangraphs.com/scouting-explained-the-20-80-scouting-scale/.
- Only 18 of the 36 schema attributes reach a match calculation. The other 18 (crossing, heading, long shots, technique, anticipation, work rate, concentration, teamwork, strength, agility, balance, jumping, handling, aerial reach, throwing, command of area, communication, rushing out) feed only the AI manager's role-fit score, or nothing. Source: crates/engine/src/player.rs:71; crates/engine/src/ai.rs:86; crates/engine/src/rules/mod.rs:730.
- Attribute effects are linear on a 1-to-100 scale: for example, top speed is a base plus a fixed amount times pace divided by 100, so the step from 90 to 100 is worth the same as the step from 50 to 60. Source: crates/engine/src/player.rs:88.
- The engine already keeps a player's base values apart from the effective values play reads, but fatigue is the only thing that lowers the effective values today. Source: crates/engine/src/player.rs:131.
- A role today is a set of attribute weights that the AI manager uses to pick a player for a slot, and a duty is only a depth offset and a risk offset. Neither changes how a player behaves with or without the ball. Source: content/tactics.json:135; content/tactics.json:205; crates/engine/src/ai.rs:86.
- The six team instructions already map to numbers the engine reads, for example pressing sets how many players press and how far they go. Source: content/tactics.json:76.

### New players entering the world

- Registration rules differ by competition: UEFA's club competitions allow 25 players on List A with 8 places for locally trained players (at least 4 club-trained, meaning three seasons between 15 and 21), plus an unlimited List B for young players; the Premier League allows 25 plus under-21s with at least 8 home-grown; Serie A 25 plus under-22s with 8 trained in Italy; the Bundesliga requires 8 locally trained players and 12 Germans with no non-EU limit; LaLiga allows 3 non-EU players and ties registration to a squad cost limit; Ligue 1 allows 4 non-EU players. Source: https://www.uefa.com/uefachampionsleague/news/02a8-216c54c7cf25-c163bc643149-1000--uefa-champions-league-league-phase-player-lists-registration/ and https://www.premierleague.com/en/news/4706140/what-are-the-rules-on-premier-league-squad-lists and https://www.bundesliga-gruppe.de/glossar/local-player-regelung/ (research sub-agent).
- England's work permits since 2021 follow a points system with up to four exception places for top-two-division clubs; foreign-player limits change over time and by country, for example Turkey moved between 5, 8, 11, and no matchday limit between 2014 and 2025, Russia rose from 8 to 13 registered foreigners in 2022, and the Netherlands sets a minimum salary for non-EU players instead of a quota. Source: https://www.thefa.com/-/media/files/thefaportal/governance-docs/registrations/gbe-2025-26/fa-mens-players-gbe-criteria-202526.ashx and https://www.aa.com.tr/tr/futbol/-turk-futbolunda-son-15-yildaki-yabanci-oyuncu-kurallari/1905742 (research sub-agent).
- About 55 percent of outfield players in the top five leagues play one position group and just under 30 percent play two, measured from real appearances; rating data suggests most midfielders are versatile, centre-backs and strikers much less so, and versatility peaks at ages 24 to 26. Source: https://www.statsandsnakeoil.com/2020/08/10/finding-the-most-versatile-player/ and the FIFA 22 player ratings (people's ratings, not measurements) (research sub-agent).
- Evidence for national playing styles is weak: league styles are measured (the Premier League most direct, the Bundesliga the most counter-attacks), but they describe leagues, not nationality; rating databases show small differences by nationality of 1 to 6 points, which may reflect raters' stereotypes and which players move abroad. Source: https://www.researchgate.net/publication/333330546 and https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1080/17461391.2010.481334 and the FIFA 22 player ratings (research sub-agent).
- No base rate exists for a professional parent or sibling: elite athletes are more than 2.3 times as likely as others to have a father who played competitive sport, and famous sons make up roughly 1 percent of World Cup 2026 squads, a lower bound. Source: https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1080/17461391.2018.1551424 and https://bolavip.com/en/world-cup/how-many-sons-of-former-players-are-participating-at-the-2026-world-cup (research sub-agent).
- Brazil, France, and Argentina export the most players (3,020, 2,293, and 2,171 across 135 leagues in 2020 to 2025); per head, Iceland, Montenegro, and Croatia export the most. The foreign share in 31 UEFA top divisions was 42.3 percent in 2022, from 7.9 percent in Ukraine to 74 percent in Cyprus, and only 17 percent of players played at the club that trained them. Source: https://www.cies.ch/research/news/news-detail/article/football-expatriates-100th-report and https://football-observatory.com/IMG/sites/mr/mr79/en/ (research sub-agent).
- Height spreads by about 6 centimetres within a league; goalkeepers average about 190 centimetres and attacking midfielders about 178; leagues differ, from over 184 centimetres in the Bundesliga to about 181 in La Liga; and English top-flight players grew about one centimetre per decade from the 1970s. Source: https://football-observatory.com/IMG/sites/mr/mr79/en/ and https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16208292/ and https://www.port.ac.uk/news-events-and-blogs/news/elite-footballers-leaner-taller-faster-than-decades-ago (research sub-agent).
- Among professionals, birth quarters run 31, 26, 24, and 20 percent, a milder lean than in academies; early-born professionals have no higher transfer values; and in one small Spanish study every player who reached a top-five league was a late maturer, so late developers who make it are a small group with high upside. Source: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371%2Fjournal.pone.0192209 and https://www.mdpi.com/2411-5142/10/2/153 (research sub-agent).
- About 79 percent of players at the 1998 World Cup were right-footed and very few used both feet equally; about 16 percent of players at the 2022 World Cup were born outside the country they played for, with more than 45 percent in the Moroccan, Tunisian, and Senegalese squads. Source: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11695507/ and https://gulfnews.com/qatar-world-cup-2022/teams/fifa-world-cup-qatar-2022-moroccos-foreign-born-contingent-deliver-1.92639526 (research sub-agent).
- No measured rate exists for a professional parent or sibling, for players who play several positions, for very tall or very short outfield players, or for national playing styles; birthplace size has an inconsistent effect, population density a clearer one, and academy players come more often from higher-income families. Source: https://jhk.termedia.pl/Birthplace-Effect-in-Soccer-A-Systematic-Review,186935,0,2.html and https://www.easm.net/download/2008/7a9ea389e692566e38fcba6483d8ae3a.pdf (research sub-agent).
- Academies run in phases: in England, a Foundation Phase from under-9 to under-11, a Youth Development Phase from under-12 to under-16, and a Professional Development Phase from under-17 to under-23; a scholarship is offered by the under-16 year and a professional contract signed at 17 at the earliest. Every German top-two club must run a graded youth centre, and French clubs use aspirant, stagiaire, and elite contracts from 15 to 20. From 2027, FIFA lets in-house trained under-18s sign contracts of up to five years. Source: https://www.premierleague.com/en/footballandcommunity/youth-development/parent-hub/rights-rules-and-responsibilities and https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nachwuchsleistungszentrum and https://www.servulo.com/en/knowledge/FIFA-Transfer-System-2027-The-New-Rules-of-The-Game/9802/ (research sub-agent).
- Few academy players make it: about 4 percent of academy players aged 13 to 18 at two LaLiga academies reached the top tier within ten years, and only one in six English players signed at 16 is still in the game at 21. Widely quoted figures such as 0.5 percent of academy entrants turning professional are not primary data. Source: https://www.essex.ac.uk/news/2024/06/27/just-four-per-cent-of-teenage-academy-players-make-it-to-top-tier-football and https://www.pressreader.com/uk/the-daily-telegraph-sport/20210330/281651077897760 (research sub-agent).
- Academies over-select players born early in the selection year: 45 to 57 percent of academy players are born in the first quarter against about 10 percent in the last, and the LaLiga study found no last-quarter births among the players who made it. Confidence, ball reception, dribbling, and coach ratings separated those who made it. Source: https://www.researchgate.net/publication/381051615 and https://www.essex.ac.uk/news/2024/06/27/just-four-per-cent-of-teenage-academy-players-make-it-to-top-tier-football (research sub-agent).
- Moving a young player between academies costs compensation: England's fixed fees rise with age and academy grade, with further payments per first-team appearance and a sell-on share; FIFA's training compensation runs from 10,000 to 90,000 euros a year by club category, and solidarity pays up to 5 percent of a later fee to the clubs that trained the player from 12 to 21. Younger English academy players must live within one hour, or 90 minutes from under-12, unless the academy houses them. Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elite_Player_Performance_Plan and https://digitalhub.fifa.com/m/6370b586e046913d/original/Circular-1892_RSTP-categorisation-of-clubs-and-registration-periods.pdf (research sub-agent).
- B teams play in the senior pyramid in Spain, the Netherlands, Portugal, and Germany, but must stay below the first team: in Spain at least one division below, in the Netherlands and Portugal with no promotion, and in Germany no higher than the 3. Liga. England uses the under-21 Premier League 2 and invitations to the EFL Trophy instead. Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primera_Federaci%C3%B3n and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eerste_Divisie and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professional_Development_League (research sub-agent).
- Academies are graded: an English Category 1 academy needs at least 18 full-time staff and a yearly budget of 2.5 million pounds, and German youth centres are graded up to three stars. Barcelona has 44 to 46 academy graduates in the big five leagues, and 30 clubs there have none. Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elite_Player_Performance_Plan and https://football-observatory.com/WeeklyPost529 (research sub-agent).

### Supporters and the press

- UEFA's club competitions require the head coach and at least one player at the press conference the day before a match, 15 minutes of training open to media, flash interviews on matchday (the head coach at least 5 and the player of the match at least 4 if asked), several players through the mixed zone, and the coach at the post-match conference. The FIFA World Cup also requires a player: Senegal were fined 10,000 Swiss francs in 2022 and Argentina 300,000 in 2014 when only the coach attended. Source: https://documents.uefa.com/r/Regulations-of-the-UEFA-Conference-League-2026/27/Article-78-Media-activities-on-the-day-before-the-match-Online and https://www.si.com/soccer/2014/07/11/world-cup-argentina-fined-skipping-interviews-alejandro-sabella (research sub-agent).
- In the leagues checked, the pre-match conference is the manager's: the Premier League names the manager only, and requires weekly player content sessions, a pre-match player interview, super-flash interviews with two players at full time, a half-time interview or dressing-room filming, at least six players through the mixed zone, the manager at the post-match conference, and a pre-season media day. The Bundesliga and Ligue 1 require club conferences without naming who attends; Ligue 1 players pass through the mixed zone but need not answer. LaLiga and Serie A rules are not confirmed. Source: https://resources.premierleague.pulselive.com/premierleague/document/2025/07/24/99839920-d274-42aa-a2ac-e5612b4f6c61/PL_Handbook_25-26_Digital_24.07.pdf and https://media.dfl.de/sites/2/2024/12/Anhang-XI-zur-LO-Medienrichtlinien-2024-12-06-Stand.pdf and https://www.lfp.fr/assets/Convention_Medias_Presse_L1_L2_2024_2029_9f1339281b.pdf (research sub-agent).
- Words in the media carry real consequences: UEFA fined Ferguson 10,000 euros in 2013 for skipping post-match media, the FA fined Chris Wilder 11,500 pounds for comments about officials, and Ronaldo's 2022 television interview ended his Manchester United contract. Source: https://www.aljazeera.com/sports/2013/3/22/uefa-fine-ferguson-and-ban-nani and https://www.cbssports.com/soccer/news/manchester-united-terminate-cristiano-ronaldo-contract-with-immediate-effect-after-explosive-interview/ (research sub-agent).

### Contracts and the dressing room

- In England the Premier League standard contract caps a fine at two weeks' basic wage for a first offence and four weeks for later offences within twelve months, unless the players' union approves more; a club can suspend a player on full pay for up to 14 days while it investigates, and the player appeals first to the club board and then to the League, with the sanction paused until the appeal ends. Source: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/579f1eef2e69cf81541f5565/t/57bc6605e58c62993057c155/1471964677468/standard+pl+contract.pdf and https://www.cnn.com/2011/10/28/sport/football/tevez-pfa-fine-manchester (research sub-agent).
- Since 2018 FIFA's transfer rules treat conduct meant to force a player to end or change a contract, such as training alone, as abusive, which gives the player just cause to leave with compensation; under a FIFA and FIFPRO agreement, the rules from 1 January 2027 expressly ban forcing a player to train in isolation or demoting a player as pressure. Source: https://www.mills-reeve.com/blogs/sports/april-2018/amendments-to-the-fifa-regulations-on-the-status-a/ and https://www.fifpro.org/en/articles/2026/06/what-the-fifpro-and-fifa-agreement-means-for-players (research sub-agent).
- Discipline limits differ widely by country: Spain caps a fine at one twentieth of annual pay and bans keeping a player out of training except as a sanction or for injury; Italy caps a fine at 25 percent of monthly pay and allows training apart only as a sanction confirmed by an arbitration board; France bans fines outright and limits separate training groups; Portugal caps a fine at one third of monthly pay per offence; the Netherlands defaults to half a day's wage per week. In Spain, Italy, and Portugal the usual remedy for abuse is ending the contract with compensation. Source: https://www.boe.es/buscar/act.php?id=BOE-A-1985-12313 and https://www.assocalciatori.it/sites/default/files/attachment/pagina/Accordo%20collettivo%20AIC%20-%20Lega%20Serie%20A.pdf and https://sjogadores.pt/uploads/files/CCT%20Atualizado.pdf and https://www.vvcs.nl/documents/2023/10/CAO-Contractspelers-2023-2027-digitaal-exemplaar.pdf (research sub-agent).
- Every source treats team selection as free: a club may leave a player out of matches without limit, and no rule limits public criticism as such, although dignity and harassment clauses in Italy, Portugal, and France come close. The fine cap for top-division German players is not public. Source: https://www.iberley.es/jurisprudencia/resolucion-contrato-trabajo-falta-ocupacion-efectiva-futbolista-profesional-sentencia-ts-sala-social-sec-1-rec-238-2008-28-04-2010-509771 and https://www.lto.de/recht/hintergruende/h/eren-derdiyok-hoffenheim-training-b-elf-beschaeftigungsanspruch-gleichbehandlung (research sub-agent).
- Few licensed agents earn fees: in 2025 there were 10,525 licensed agents, and only about 15 to 17 percent were paid in an international deal. Deals paying more than one million US dollars were about 12 percent of paid cases and 68 percent of the money. Source: https://inside.fifa.com/news/club-agent-service-fees-reach-new-heights-2025 (FIFA Football Agents Report 2025) (research sub-agent).
- The agent market is concentrated: in 2010/11 half of big-five players were clients of 83 agents or agencies and a quarter of 24, placed through closed networks with clubs; in 2025 two agencies each represent more than 100 big-five players. Source: https://football-observatory.com/IMG/pdf/report_agents_2012-2.pdf and https://football-observatory.com/Leading-agencies-in-the-big-5-ongoing (research sub-agent).
- Agents gain from moves more than renewals: the usual dual role is the player plus the buying club (851 of 962 double representations in 2025), and FIFA's rules, largely upheld by the EU Court of Justice in July 2026, cap fees at 10 percent of the transfer fee from the selling club and 3 to 5 percent of wages from the player or buying club. Source: https://inside.fifa.com/news/club-agent-service-fees-reach-new-heights-2025 and https://www.ashurstperkinscoie.com/en/insights/ecj-rules-on-fifa-agent-regulations-compatibility-with-eu-competition-law/ (research sub-agent).
- Agents recruit young players heavily: in 2011, 58 percent of agents' clients were not senior professionals, and FIFA's rules let an agent approach a minor only in the six months before the first professional contract, with a guardian's written consent. No study measures how often players change agents. Source: https://football-observatory.com/IMG/pdf/report_agents_2012-2.pdf and https://digitalhub.fifa.com/m/1e7b741fa0fae779/original/FIFA-Football-Agent-Regulations.pdf (research sub-agent).
- A contract runs at most five years, or three for a player under 18, and a protected period of three years (signed before 28) or two years (signed at 28 or older) bans a player who breaks the contract for four months. Squads in the big five leagues average about 2.3 years left on their contracts. Source: https://jusmundi.com/en/document/rule/en-fifa-federation-internationale-de-football-association-regulations-on-the-status-and-transfer-of-players-2025-fifa-rstp-2025-tuesday-1st-july-2025 and https://football-observatory.com/IMG/sites/b5wp/2017/222/en/ (research sub-agent).
- Spanish law requires a release clause in every player contract, and Brazil has a similar system; in England, Germany, and France a release clause is optional. No figures show how common or how large release clauses are elsewhere. Source: https://confilegal.com/20200120-la-clausula-de-rescision-por-voluntad-del-futbolista-en-espana-es-unica-en-inglaterra-alemania-o-francia-no-existe/ (research sub-agent).
- Free transfers are common: 26 to 32 percent of signings in the big five leagues in 2019 and 2020, and 62.5 percent of international transfers in 2025, which carried 0.8 percent of fee value; only 17.7 percent of international transfers had a fee. Source: https://football-observatory.com/IMG/sites/mr/mr58/en/ and the FIFA Global Transfer Report 2025 (research sub-agent).
- Agent fees are large: clubs paid agents 1.37 billion US dollars on international transfers in 2025, about 10 percent of transfer spending, and Premier League clubs pay about 400 to 460 million pounds a year. FIFA suspended its caps on agent fees at the end of 2023. Source: https://inside.fifa.com/transfer-system/agents/news/club-agent-service-fees-reach-new-heights-2025 and https://www.thefa.com/news/2026/apr/01/agent-fees-and-transactions (research sub-agent).
- A relegation wage cut is reported as usually 25 to 30 percent, within a range of 20 to 50 percent; no count shows how many contracts carry one. Source: https://www.foxsports.com/articles/soccer/revealed-tottenham-stars-would-have-wages-cut-by-50-per-cent-if-they-suffer-premier-league-relegation (research sub-agent).
- Promises about playing time, squad status, or signings appear to be verbal only, not contract terms; the only written remedy is that an established player who plays in fewer than 10 percent of official matches may end the contract, and rulings on it are rare. Signing-on and loyalty payments are paid in equal yearly instalments in the Premier League. Source: https://www.football-legal.com/content/a-football-rarity-an-article-15-rstp-sporting-just-cause-case-at-the-cas and https://www.espn.co.uk/football/story/_/id/38291291/whats-contract-salary-add-ons-transfer-fee-more (research sub-agent).
- No published figures give the spread of contract lengths at signing, the share of pay that bonuses make up, how common each clause is, or how often loyalty bonuses and signing-on fees appear. Source: the research sub-agent found no published figure.

### The staff around the squad

- Staff headcount falls steeply by tier: a Bundesliga club employs about 350 full-time staff and a 2. Bundesliga club about 158, so a second-tier club has about 45 percent of a top-tier club's staff (DFL, 2024/25). A European top-division club averages about 130 full-time-equivalent staff (UEFA, 2024), while large Premier League clubs employ 600 to more than 1,200 people. Source: https://report.bundesliga.de/2425/de/wirtschaftsreport/wirtschaftliche-kennzahlen/lizenzfussball/jobs.html and https://ecfil.uefa.com/2025 (research sub-agent).
- Specialist roles fall off steeply below the top: a top club runs a median of 5 match analysts and 3 sports scientists (32 clubs, 2024); full-time nutritionists work at 14 of 26 Category 1 English academies and at 1 of 63 lower-category academies; and only 14 percent of English non-league clubs have a sports scientist and 4 percent a nutritionist. Source: https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/full/10.1080/24733938.2024.2341837 and https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35300580/ and https://doi.org/10.3390/app15031050 (research sub-agent).
- Performance staff pay falls by division: Premier League fitness and sports-science staff earn about 6,300 pounds more than Championship staff and about 21,800 pounds more than League Two staff (138 staff surveyed, about 2023). Non-player wages across UEFA top-division clubs reached 5.2 billion euros in 2024, with no split by tier. Source: https://www.sportsmith.co/reports/british-football-performance-staff-survey-2/ (research sub-agent).
- About three coaches leave with each sacked manager: in one recent English season about 150 coaches lost their jobs alongside about 50 dismissed managers. Some managers bring five or more assistants to a new club, and others hire new staff at each club. No study gives the share of staff who move with a manager, and the season of the English figure needs a check. Source: https://www.aol.com/articles/more-200-managers-coaches-lost-144321000.html and https://archive.trainingground.guru/articles/why-norwich-are-defying-convention-with-new-backroom-team (research sub-agent).
- No published figures give average staff headcount for English divisions below the Premier League or for the 3. Liga, how common set-piece coaches, loan managers, or data scientists are by division, or non-player wages by tier. Source: the research sub-agent found no published figure.

### The in-progress plans and what they miss

- The board believed the engine bounced the ball off the touchlines and the goal lines like walls, with no throw-ins, corners, goal kicks, or offside. The code now shows otherwise: the protocol's event list (crates/protocol/src/event.rs) carries throw-in, corner, goal-kick, and offside events. Source: crates/protocol/src/event.rs:25 (throw-in, corner, goal-kick, and offside events now exist).
- This board runs ahead of the build: the football-manager-match-engine workflow is at the plan and implement stages of its stream-protocol slice, so every candidate here lands as later scope and none corrects work already in flight. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/00-index.md:9.
- The match-engine build workflow places leagues, seasons, competitions, transfers, and finances out of scope, and it parks injury persistence because no season model exists. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/02-shape.md:239.
- The stream-protocol slice is complete and four later slices depend on it, so the event contract is a second completed contract, after the player record, that the realism work wants changed. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/00-index.md:137.
- The engine is done when a person can watch and manage one match from start to finish, exactly as the sixteen-slice roster describes; calling the product a football-management game does not change the engine's definition of done. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/00-index.md:134.

### The player as a person: attributes, personality, body, positions

- The board believed the attribute schema held 37 attributes in four groups, with no personality attribute, no hidden trait, and no positional-familiarity value. The schema file shows 36 attributes, nine per group, when it was read again in session 5. Source: content/attributes.json:1 (36 attributes, nine per group, at session 5).
- A player record carries an identifier, a name, a shirt number, one position code, and attributes. It carries no age, no preferred foot, no height, and no starting condition. Source: crates/engine/src/data/team.rs:89.
- A player has exactly one position code, drawn from ten: GK, CB, LB, RB, DM, CM, AM, LW, RW, ST. Source: crates/engine/src/data/team.rs:17.
- The board believed the attribute schema accepts 30 to 50 attributes in four fixed groups with 13 free slots, so any personality attribute would have to be declared in an existing group such as mental. The schema file holds 36 attributes, so 14 slots are free, as read in session 5. Source: content/attributes.json:1 (36 attributes, so 14 slots are free, at session 5).
- An outfield player covers 9.6 to 10.3 kilometres per match, spends about 70 percent of the time below 2 metres per second, and sprints above 7 metres per second for 0.25 to 0.44 percent of the match, covering 100 to 200 metres at that speed. Measured from two Metrica matches of 25-frames-per-second tracking plus events. Source: .scratch/out/metrica2.json (2 matches, 25 fps tracking plus events).
- Peak sprint velocity has a narrow spread across a league: the tenth percentile is 26.6 kilometres per hour, the median 28.3, the ninetieth percentile 29.9, and the fastest player 31.8. Measured from 20 A-League matches of 2024/25 SkillCorner tracking. Source: .scratch/data/skillcorner (20 A-League matches 2024/25).
- Players cover more high-speed distance out of possession than in it, 1052 against 805 metres per 90 minutes for high-speed running and 261 against 212 metres for sprinting, so defending costs more at high intensity than attacking. Measured from 20 A-League matches of 2024/25 SkillCorner tracking. Source: .scratch/data/skillcorner (20 A-League matches 2024/25).
- Every outfield role covers 26 to 43 metres of the pitch's length between its tenth and ninetieth percentile. Attacking midfielders roam widest laterally at 42.9 metres while full-backs stay narrowest at about 30, and central midfielders sit nearest the ball at 18.7 to 19.6 metres and within 20 metres of it for 56 to 58 percent of the match. Measured from 20 SkillCorner matches, 3.87 million player samples. Source: .scratch/out/sc_player_frames.csv.gz (20 matches, 3.87M samples).
- Players move about twice as fast within 5 metres of the ball as beyond 40 metres, and attackers show the steepest gradient: a striker runs 3.62 metres per second near the ball and 1.81 far from it, against a centre-back's 2.86 and 1.87. Measured from two Metrica matches at 25 frames per second. Source: .scratch/out/metrica_roles.csv (2 matches at 25 fps).
- An outfielder covers 10.25 kilometres per ninety minutes on official Bundesliga tracking, and the difference between roles lives in intensity rather than volume: a centre-back covers 84 percent of a wing-back's total distance but only 46 percent of the wing-back's high-intensity distance, 276 metres against 595 per half. Measured from 7 DFL matches, 247 full player-halves at 25 frames per second. Source: .scratch/out/idsse_players.csv (7 DFL matches, 247 full player-halves at 25 fps).
- A player sprints above 7 metres per second for 0.7 percent of live time and runs below 2 metres per second for 45 percent of it, and no more than about one outfielder in twenty exceeds 5.5 metres per second at any instant, so top speed is an attribute the match almost never uses. Measured from 7 DFL matches, 247 full player-halves at 25 frames per second. Source: .scratch/out/idsse_players.csv (7 DFL matches, 247 full player-halves at 25 fps).
- Every outfield role runs faster without the ball than with it, 2.49 metres per second against 2.20 for centre-backs and 2.86 against 2.59 for defensive midfielders, so defending costs more running than attacking and costs the deepest roles the most. Measured from 7 DFL matches, 247 full player-halves at 25 frames per second. Source: .scratch/out/idsse_players.csv (7 DFL matches, 247 full player-halves at 25 fps).
- Pass completion is a property of pitch position rather than of the person: among comparable players in one pair of matches, flat-pass completion runs from 98.6 percent for goalkeepers and 95 for centre-backs down to 73.2 for centre-forwards, so a uniform passing attribute would make defenders too error-prone and forwards too accurate. Measured from 1650 DFL passes joined to tracking at 9 milliseconds. Source: .scratch/out/idsse_sync.csv (1650 passes joined to tracking at 9 ms).
- The late-match fall in running is dead time rather than tired legs. From minutes 0 to 30 to minutes 60 to 90, distance per minute falls 17 percent and high-speed running per minute 26 percent, but the ball is in play 27 percent less, and high-speed running per minute of live play does not fall at all; forwards are the one exception, at minus 12 percent. Measured from 7 DFL matches with tracking joined to events, recorded in the findings document. Source: .scratch/out/gap_idsse_an.txt (7 DFL matches, tracking joined to events) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- The attribute schema already carries composure, aggression, work rate, teamwork, and injury resistance in its mental and physical groups. Source: content/attributes.json:1.
- Skill is small beside the situation for passing, shooting, and saving, and large for duels. After the situation and binomial noise are removed, players spread by 3.6 points of pass completion against an 18.3-point situational spread, and by 2.5 points of shot conversion and 2.4 of save rate with split-half correlations of only 0.17 and 0.14; but they spread by 10.0 points in aerial duels against a 5.8-point situational spread, and by 7.6 in attacking ground duels. Measured from 1826 Wyscout league matches, recorded in the findings document. Source: .scratch/out/gap_wy.txt (1826 Wyscout league matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- About 21 to 27 percent of elite players are left-footed, and players use the dominant foot for about 80 percent of actions. Professionals show no significant success gap between their feet where amateurs do, and two-footed players earned a wage premium of about 20 percent with full controls. No real-data share of two-footed players exists; EA's ratings put about 1.3 to 1.8 percent of its database at the top weak-foot level. Sources: a London School of Economics Centre for Economic Performance paper and a Psychology Today article, found by a research sub-agent. Source: https://cep.lse.ac.uk/pubs/download/cp312.pdf and https://www.psychologytoday.com/us/blog/the-asymmetric-brain/202212/messi-magic-left-footedness-in-professional-soccer-players (research sub-agent).

### The club and the squad: identity, venue, mood, cohesion

- A team file today carries a club identifier, a name, a short name, and two kit colours; it carries no reputation, no home ground, and no crowd data, so two clubs differ only in their players and every fixture is played on neutral ground. Source: content/teams/default-a.json:1.
- The share of time the ball is above 1 metre differs between competitions, 12.4 percent in European elite matches against 19.4 percent in the A-League (measured on 20 SkillCorner A-League matches from 2024/25), so ball height is a measurable signal of playing identity rather than a constant. Source: .scratch/data/skillcorner (20 A-League matches 2024/25).
- Club-to-club style varies far more than league-to-league: across 98 clubs in 1826 Wyscout matches from five leagues, pass accuracy spans 73 to 90 percent against 1.1 percentage points between leagues, pass volume varies 2.57 times, mean pass length spans 17.9 to 23.0 metres, and shot conversion spans 4.9 to 17.5 percent. Source: .scratch/out/wyscout_rates.json (1826 matches, five leagues).
- Physical output separates neither divisions nor clubs: in seven DFL matches (247 full player-halves tracked at 25 frames per second), Bundesliga outfielders cover 110.0 metres per minute against 115.4 in the second division, top speed and sprint share are identical between the divisions, and across fourteen team-matches the spread is 20 percent, against the 73 to 90 percent club-to-club spread in pass accuracy measured across 98 clubs. Source: .scratch/out/idsse_players.csv (7 DFL matches, 247 full player-halves at 25 fps).
- When two styles meet, the measured additive possession rule (match possession equals 0.487 plus 0.949 times the difference in the two clubs' season means, plus 0.027 at home) is the default, and the manager's tactic for that match moves the result away from it, so a possession club may choose to give the ball up in one match; the realism test bands the default. The Wyscout data verifies the default rule only; the effect of the tactic is unverified, and the default was later made an emergent output rather than a rule applied to each match. Source: .scratch/out/gap_wy.txt:166 (the default rule only; the tactic effect is unverified).
- Team strength has a measured size in 1826 Wyscout league matches: the best attack in a league scores about twice the league average and the worst about 0.6 times it, the home factor runs from 1.19 to 1.35, 71 to 84 percent of the spread in points per game is team quality, a top-quarter club beats a bottom-half club 71.0 percent of the time and loses 10.4 percent, and evenly matched games draw 4 to 6 points more often than two independent Poisson scorers predict. Source: .scratch/out/gap_wy.txt (1826 Wyscout league matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- Club style has two layers: one axis carries 59 percent of style variance and is mostly strength, with possession correlating 0.74 with points per game, while crosses and take-ons are almost independent of strength. The opponent sets match possession as much as the club does, since match possession equals 0.487 plus 0.949 times the difference in the two clubs' season means plus 0.027 at home, and a club's possession swings 10.3 points from match to match against a 7.2-point spread between clubs (1826 Wyscout league matches). Source: .scratch/out/gap_wy.txt (1826 Wyscout league matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- Quality scales as small execution gaps in every family of action: seven DFL matches show no measurable gap between the Bundesliga and the 2. Bundesliga, while inside one league (1826 Wyscout matches) the top quarter of clubs beats its situational expectation by 1.7 points of pass completion, 2.0 of shot conversion, and 2.5 in duels and take-ons, and the bottom quarter falls short by 1.2, 2.1, and 1.2. Source: .scratch/out/gap_wy.txt (1826 Wyscout league matches) and FINDINGS.md §16; verified .scratch/out/gap_div.txt.

### Tactics beyond Tier 2: phases, transitions, set pieces, live changes

- The planned tactics model is Tier 2: a formation, a mentality, six team instructions, and a role and duty for each player, as written in the tactics-and-AI slice of the match-engine workflow. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/03-slice-tactics-and-ai.md:41.
- Real phase-of-play data uses nine paired phases rather than two: build-up, create, finish, direct, transition, quick break, set play, chaotic, and disruption, each locked one-to-one to the opponent's high block, medium block, low block, and the matching defending phases. Measured on SkillCorner data from 20 A-League matches of 2024/25. Source: .scratch/data/skillcorner (20 A-League matches 2024/25).
- The two phases differ by a measurable amount: in possession a team is 46 to 49 metres wide with a defensive line 34 to 37 metres up the pitch, and out of possession 36 to 38 metres wide with the line at 31 to 35 metres, while depth barely changes. Measured on Metrica tracking and event data from 2 matches at 25 frames per second. Source: .scratch/out/metrica2.json (2 matches, 25 fps tracking plus events).
- The shape does not move uniformly between phases: on broadcast tracking from 20 matches (3.87 million samples), when a team wins the ball the back line advances about 3.5 metres while full-backs, wing-backs, and wingers advance 9 to 12 metres, so the team stretches from the back rather than sliding. Ground-truth tracking later halved these magnitudes and reversed the back line's direction. Source: .scratch/out/sc_player_frames.csv.gz (20 matches, 3.87M samples).
- The block tracks the ball at a fixed ratio, about 0.63 metres up the pitch for every metre the ball advances and 0.30 to 0.39 metres sideways for every metre it moves across, with the same slope in and out of possession; what changes between phases is width, 45.1 metres in possession against 35.4 out of it. Measured on SkillCorner broadcast data from 20 matches (1.76 million team-frames). Source: .scratch/out/sc_team_frames.csv.gz (20 matches, 1.76M team-frames).
- Only 1.62 of the twenty outfielders stand within 5 metres of the ball and 3.71 within 10, the ten-metre zone is balanced at 1.84 attackers to 1.87 defenders, and 51.5 percent of live frames hold at most one player inside 5 metres, so the ball is uncontested more often than it is contested (2 Bundesliga matches, 154 thousand live frames). These figures were corrected after a position-code fix restored two wingers; the first version read 1.55, 3.55, 1.77 to 1.78, and 54.8 percent. Source: .scratch/out/idsse_crowd.csv (2 Bundesliga matches, 154k live frames).
- Ground-truth tracking confirms the block law first measured on broadcast data: the team centroid moves 0.558 metres up the pitch per metre of ball advance in possession and 0.555 out of it, and 0.35 and 0.39 sideways, staying within 0.44 to 0.68 across 52 team-halves from 7 German league (DFL) matches, while width changes from 46.1 metres in possession to 36.8 out and depth changes by under 3 metres. Source: .scratch/out/idsse_shape.csv (52 team-halves, 7 DFL matches).
- Ground-truth tracking corrects the broadcast finding that the back line advances about 3.5 metres and the wide players 9 to 12 metres when a team wins the ball. The ordering survives, with wide midfielders advancing 7.4 metres and full-backs 5.5 against a centre-back's zero, but the magnitudes halve and the back line moves the other way: the deepest outfielder drops 1.4 metres deeper while the centroid advances 2.5, so the team stretches rather than sliding forward (52 team-halves, 7 DFL matches). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_shape.csv (52 team-halves, 7 DFL matches).
- Both managers decide live: the AI manager changes its tactic during the match, the human can pause and change as well, and the human's pre-set triggers for pressing, the defensive line, and similar situations are an extra tool rather than the only one. The code confirms the protocol already queues a tactics change or a substitution during a match (crates/protocol/src/command.rs); the AI side is not yet verified. Source: crates/protocol/src/command.rs:1 and crates/protocol/src/command.rs:11 (the change queue; the AI side is unverified).
- Every live change, tactical or a substitution, takes effect at the next stoppage, for both managers; the plan already applies each queued change at a qualifying stoppage through the match-rules slice, so pausing gives the human time to think but no earlier effect. Source: crates/protocol/src/command.rs:2.
- Goals come from both short and long possessions: possessions of 0 to 2 passes give 34 percent of goals and possessions of 10 or more give 25; a ball won in the final third leads to a shot 27.0 percent of the time against 9.7 in the own third; 34.1 percent of the 200 open-play losses a match are regained within 5 seconds; and a SkillCorner quick break leads to a shot 46 percent of the time. Measured on 200 StatsBomb matches and 20 SkillCorner matches. Source: .scratch/out/gap_sb.txt (200 StatsBomb matches) and FINDINGS.md §16; verified .scratch/out/gap_sc.txt (20 SkillCorner matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- Pressing has measured triggers and the block a measured shape: a player receiving a forward pass is engaged 88.5 percent of the time against 59.5 after a backward pass, 78 percent when wide against 56 in the centre, and 82 percent against a low block; the defending block keeps a near-constant length of about 30 metres, its last defender advancing 0.49 metres and its midfield line 0.61 per metre of ball advance. Measured on 20 SkillCorner matches and 7 DFL matches with tracking joined to events. Source: .scratch/out/gap_sc.txt (20 SkillCorner matches) and FINDINGS.md §16; verified .scratch/out/gap_idsse_an.txt (7 DFL matches, tracking joined to events) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- Marking tightens near goal and hands over quickly: the nearest defending outfielder stands a median 2.4 metres from an attacker in the box-depth zone against 6.3 metres far from goal, and the nearest defender is still the same player 5 seconds later 64.0 percent of the time overall but only 51.3 percent near goal and 37.8 percent over 10 seconds. Measured on 7 DFL matches with tracking joined to events. Source: .scratch/out/gap_idsse_an.txt (7 DFL matches, tracking joined to events) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- Set pieces are first-contact contests: a corner leads to a goal 3.45 percent of the time and to a shot 38.0 percent, the attacking team wins first contact on 37.2 percent of long corners and then shoots 68.9 percent of the time against 20.5 when the defenders win it, corners, free kicks, and penalties together give 36 percent of goals, and a direct free kick scores 3.2 percent of the time. Measured on 200 StatsBomb matches. Source: .scratch/out/gap_sb.txt (200 StatsBomb matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.

### How the match plays: actions, choices, duels, the ball in the air, officials, injuries

- The tactics-and-ai slice of the engine build carries a benchmark tripwire: it fails if CPU time per match rises more than 10 percent, or memory more than 25 percent, over the match-rules baseline. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/03-slice-tactics-and-ai.md:73.
- The ball already carries a three-dimensional position and velocity, so height exists in the engine today and an aerial model has somewhere to live. Source: crates/engine/src/ball.rs:9.
- A real match carries about 1683 events. Wyscout types them with 10 types and about 35 subtypes, Metrica with 9 types and subtypes, and kloppy's interoperable model with 19 types (measured over 1826 Wyscout matches from five leagues in 2017/18). Source: .scratch/out/wyscout_rates.json (1826 matches, five leagues 2017/18).
- Pass completion depends on the kind of pass rather than on one rate: simple passes complete 91.7 percent, headers 62.5, launches 53.8, high passes 53.7, through balls 39.2, and crosses 30.7 percent (1826 Wyscout matches, five leagues, 2017/18). Source: .scratch/out/wyscout_rates.json (1826 matches, five leagues 2017/18).
- Metrica tracking agrees with the completion-by-pass-kind figures when passes are split by length: under 10 metres 82 to 89 percent, 10 to 25 metres 92 percent, 25 to 40 metres 70 to 72 percent, and over 40 metres 58 percent (2 matches, 25 frames per second tracking plus events). Source: .scratch/out/metrica2.json (2 matches, 25 fps tracking plus events).
- The duel is the most common contested action in football at 456 a match, 87 of them aerial, and the engine models no duel at all (1826 Wyscout matches, five leagues, 2017/18). Source: .scratch/out/wyscout_rates.json (1826 matches, five leagues 2017/18).
- The ball sits above 1 metre for 12.4 percent of tracked frames in nine European top-league matches and 19.4 percent in twenty A-League matches, and above 2 metres for 7.6 and 10.6 percent, reaching 12 to 15 metres at its highest (SkillCorner tracking, A-League 2024/25). Source: .scratch/data/skillcorner (20 A-League matches 2024/25).
- Ball height is standard in tracking data rather than exotic: kloppy (version 3.19.0) types every tracking frame's ball position as a three-dimensional point and fills in the height for Tracab, Sportec, StatsPerform, SkillCorner, PFF, Second Spectrum, Signality, and HawkEye. Source: kloppy v3.19.0 kloppy/domain/models/tracking.py:38.
- The minimum event fields that kloppy and floodlight agree on are the event type, the period and timestamp, the start coordinates and (where the event has an end) the end coordinates, the team identifier, the player identifier, and an outcome. Source: kloppy v3.19.0 kloppy/domain/models/event.py:686.
- The ball is in play for 59 percent of recorded frames, about 55 minutes, and its speed has a median of 5.9 metres per second, a ninetieth percentile of 15.1, and a maximum near 57 (2 Metrica matches, 25 frames per second). Source: .scratch/out/metrica2.json (2 matches, 25 fps tracking plus events).
- Most possession is brief: a match holds about 475 possession chains, 67.8 percent of them carry no pass or one pass, the median chain lasts 2.9 seconds, and only one chain in 20 ends in a shot (2 Metrica matches). Source: .scratch/out/metrica2.json (2 matches, 25 fps tracking plus events).
- A player on the ball has an opponent within 5 metres for 56 percent of touches, with the nearest opponent a median 4.4 metres away, and has on average only 1.16 teammates within 10 metres (3366 StatsBomb 360 freeze frames). Source: .scratch/data/sb360/f360.json (3366 freeze frames).
- High intensity concentrates into attacking sequences rather than spreading across the match: during goal-scoring sequences players average 3.16 metres per second and spend 8.8 percent of the time above 7 metres per second, against a match average of 1.67 and 0.25 to 0.44 percent (19 goal sequences at 20 frames per second, Last Row data). Source: .scratch/data/lastrow (19 goal sequences at 20 fps).
- Across all passes, pressure appears not to matter, moving completion by only seven points and not in order, because the purpose of a pass is confounded with pressure; restricted to flat passes the curve is monotone and spans twenty points, from 79.8 percent with an opponent inside two metres to 100 percent beyond twelve (1650 passes joined to tracking within 9 milliseconds). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_sync.csv (1650 passes joined to tracking at 9 ms).
- The duel is the second most common on-ball event at 201.7 a match in DFL data, which reconciles the earlier figure of 456 a match because Wyscout logs one row per participant while DFL logs one per contest; a duel turns the ball over in only 29.4 percent of cases (7 DFL matches, 9961 events). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_events.csv.gz (7 DFL matches, 9961 events).
- The ground duel is sharply asymmetric: when the ball carrier wins, possession changes hands 6.4 percent of the time, and when the defender wins it changes 51.4 percent, while the aerial duel is nearly symmetric at 31.2 against 36.6 percent, so a model with one win probability and an automatic turnover would roughly double real turnover rates (7 DFL matches). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_events.csv.gz (7 DFL matches, 9961 events).
- Fouls are an outcome of the duel rather than a separate mechanism: 10.6 percent of duels resolve as a foul, giving 21.4 a match against the 22.9 foul events the schema records, so an engine that models duels obtains fouls, free kicks, and cautions without a separate foul generator (7 DFL matches). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_events.csv.gz (7 DFL matches, 9961 events).
- The duel looked dominant partly because the DFL schema files several action families inside one element: take-ons at 14.6 a match with 65.7 percent success, shielding as retained control at 17.6, and beaten defenders at 7.3 all live inside the TacklingGame element, so its 201.7 contests a match are a container rather than a single action (7 DFL matches). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_events.csv.gz (7 DFL matches, 9961 events).
- Evidence now covers the pass, through ball, cross, shot, dribble, duel, shot block, positioning, and aggregate off-ball movement, while three families have no outcome evidence at all: the carry, which no schema read records as an event; the ten off-ball run types that SkillCorner names but nothing has counted; and shielding outside a recorded duel. Source: .scratch/out/idsse_events.csv.gz (7 DFL matches, 9961 events).
- The full action set is 53 actions, 38 outfield and 15 goalkeeper; public data measures 39 in full and 10 partially, and feints only thinly, and nothing public measures marking and cover assignments, keeper organisation, or keeper dive movement. Source: .scratch/out/ACTIONS.md.
- The carry is measured on three providers: 273 to 287 carries of at least 5 metres a match (corrected from an earlier 271), at about 4 metres per second, with 79.9 percent kept, longer carries faster and riskier, and centre-backs carrying the most because they receive with the most space (7 DFL matches among the sources). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_control_segments.csv and idsse_gk_positions.csv.gz (7 DFL matches).
- Pressure at the moment of receiving is the common input across actions: from no pressure to very high pressure, possession loss rises from 1.0 to 32.6 percent, pass completion falls from 93.4 to 67.0 percent, and one-touch play rises from 5.6 to 31.3 percent; DFL tracking gives the same gradient from geometry alone, 93.4 percent kept with no opponent inside 10 metres against 60.9 with one inside 1 metre (20 SkillCorner matches). Source: .scratch/out/sc_dynamic_events.csv.gz (20 SkillCorner matches) and ACTIONS.md.
- Off-ball runs happen 495 times a match across ten subtypes, and a run's danger and its completion move in opposite directions: safe runs such as coming short and dropping off are received 91 to 98 percent of the time when targeted and almost never lead to a shot, while runs in behind and runs to receive a cross are received 40 to 52 percent of the time and lead to a shot 20 to 29 percent of the time (20 SkillCorner matches). Source: .scratch/out/sc_dynamic_events.csv.gz (20 SkillCorner matches) and ACTIONS.md.
- A single pressing engagement is credited with a regain only 18.5 percent of the time, yet 80.6 percent of pressing chains end in a regain, and pressing in the opponent's third regains 32.6 percent against 25.4 in midfield, so the value of pressing is collective (200 StatsBomb matches). Source: .scratch/out/sb_events.csv.gz (200 StatsBomb matches) and ACTIONS.md.
- Bodies between the ball and the goal decide shots: with no defender in the shot cone a shot scores 12.4 percent of the time and is blocked 20.9 percent, with two defenders it scores 5.4 and is blocked 40.4, and a shooter has an opponent inside 5 metres 88.2 percent of the time (4671 non-penalty shots with StatsBomb 360 frames). Source: .scratch/out/sb360_actions.csv (4671 non-penalty shots with 360 frames).
- A keeper's save rate falls with shot quality, from 89.1 percent below 0.05 expected goals to 27.2 percent above 0.4; two saves in three are not held (a fifth go behind and a fifth are parried into danger), and 87 percent of goals are conceded without the keeper touching the ball (200 StatsBomb matches). Source: .scratch/out/sb_events.csv.gz (200 StatsBomb matches) and ACTIONS.md.
- Keeper depth follows a measured rule: out of possession with the ball in the keeper's own half, depth is about 0.23 metres per metre of ball distance from goal, the keeper spends 42 percent of live time beyond the depth of the box, shifts 0.17 metres sideways per metre the ball moves across, and stands a median 2.2 metres off the line at the moment of a shot, where depth has no effect on conversion once shot quality is known (7 DFL matches). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_control_segments.csv and idsse_gk_positions.csv.gz (7 DFL matches).
- Keepers distribute 66 times a match between them at 73.5 percent completion, with short passes and throws above 97 percent and punts at 29.1; short goal kicks are 45.3 percent of goal kicks and complete at 99.4 percent against 44.1 for long ones, and the 2019 goal-kick law halved the median goal kick from 65.6 metres in 2017/18 to 31.3 metres in 2020 to 2024 (200 StatsBomb matches). Source: .scratch/out/sb_events.csv.gz (200 StatsBomb matches) and ACTIONS.md.
- Dribbles happen 27.4 times a match at 53.0 percent success, success falls up the pitch from 61.5 percent in a team's own third to 48.2 in the final third, and role changes how often a player dribbles but not how often the dribble succeeds: wingers attempt six times as many as centre-backs, at about the same success rate as full-backs and forwards (200 StatsBomb matches). Source: .scratch/out/sb_events.csv.gz (200 StatsBomb matches) and ACTIONS.md.
- Shielding is common but barely coded: event feeds record 1.3 shields a match, while tracking shows about 80 possessions a match held against an opponent inside 2 metres, 10 of them stationary, with about two in three kept (7 DFL matches). Source: .scratch/out/idsse_control_segments.csv and idsse_gk_positions.csv.gz (7 DFL matches).
- The decision to shoot is a steep threshold at the edge of the box: 87.7 percent of on-ball releases inside 6 metres are shots, 72.1 at 6 to 11 metres, 35.3 at 11 to 16.5, 20.2 at 16.5 to 22, and 6.2 at 22 to 30, and pressure halves the shooting rate outside 11 metres; under pressure the share of releases that are passes falls from 85.5 to 52.9 percent as clearances, dribbles, and dispossessions appear (200 StatsBomb matches). Source: .scratch/out/gap_sb.txt (200 StatsBomb matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- Players choose the safe open option more often than the dangerous one: over 70,059 passes with freeze frames, a four-term score (space around the receiver, clearance of the passing lane, distance, and forward progress) picks the real receiver 55.5 percent of the time against 14.9 by chance, and among SkillCorner's tracked options the safest is chosen 45.0 percent of the time and the most threatening 31.7, below the 36.0 chance rate (200 StatsBomb matches and 20 SkillCorner matches). Source: .scratch/out/gap_sb.txt (200 StatsBomb matches) and FINDINGS.md §16; verified .scratch/out/gap_sc.txt (20 SkillCorner matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- The match changes with the clock: minutes 75 to 90 produce goals 41 percent faster than minutes 0 to 15 and added time 70 percent faster, passing tempo falls from 10.3 to 8.2 passes a minute and to 7.0 in added time, and cautions rise fivefold (1826 Wyscout league matches). Source: .scratch/out/gap_wy.txt (1826 Wyscout league matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- The score changes behaviour once team strength is removed: a team trailing by one goal scores at 1.10 times its own expected rate and a team leading by one at 0.92; the trailing team's possession rises above its expectation from 2 points early to 12 points after the 90th minute; the leading team takes its throw-ins, goal kicks, and free kicks at a median 22.0 seconds against 15.1 for the trailing team; and the team that scores first wins 69.4 percent of the time (1826 Wyscout matches, strength-adjusted, and 7 DFL matches). Source: .scratch/out/gap_wy_state.txt (1826 Wyscout league matches, strength-adjusted) and FINDINGS.md §16; verified .scratch/out/gap_idsse_an.txt (7 DFL matches, tracking joined to events) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- On the ground, local numbers decide duels: with all 1,412 DFL duels joined to tracking, the defender wins 42.7 percent when outnumbered within 5 metres, 44.2 when level, 53.2 with one extra teammate, and 66.7 with two, while in the air support makes no difference once the defender is not outnumbered (7 DFL matches, tracking joined to events). Source: .scratch/out/gap_idsse_an.txt (7 DFL matches, tracking joined to events) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- Loose balls are a real phase: a clearance is next controlled by the clearing team only 26.5 percent of the time, the team of a player who wins a header controls the next ball only 48.0 percent of the time against 39.3 for the loser's team, and 6.2 percent of shots are rebounds within 5 seconds, which convert at 16.4 percent against 11.3 (200 StatsBomb matches). Source: .scratch/out/gap_sb.txt (200 StatsBomb matches) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- The ball is dead for 42.2 minutes a match, and each restart has a measured stoppage: a throw-in takes a median 13.8 seconds, a goal kick 23.2, a corner 31.8, a free kick 32.5, and the kick-off after a goal 68.2 (7 DFL matches, tracking joined to events). Source: .scratch/out/gap_idsse_an.txt (7 DFL matches, tracking joined to events) and FINDINGS.md §16.
- Referees favour the home side measurably: in Serie A 2007-08 matches played behind closed doors, home bias was estimated at 23 percent for fouls, 26 percent for yellow cards, and 70 percent for red cards; Champions League referees gave away teams 25 percent more yellow cards than home teams and Europa League referees 10 percent more, after controlling for dominance. Source: https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0165176510001497 and https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/02640414.2017.1377911 (research sub-agent).
- The crowd drives the referee's leaning: in Bundesliga matches without spectators during COVID-19, the home team's foul advantage of about 0.6 a match reversed to about one foul against, home cards rose significantly, the bias scales with crowd size, and home advantage in results shrank but did not vanish. Source: https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0165176520303815 and https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8011816/ (research sub-agent).
- Added time leans towards the home side: referees add about 44 seconds less when the home team leads by one goal than when the away team does, about 60 seconds less than an unbiased benchmark with fans present, and the gap disappears without a crowd (from a study summary only). Source: https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2773161825000011 (summary only) (research sub-agent).
- A video assistant referee overturns a decision about once every three to four matches: 109 overturns in the 2019-20 Premier League season from about 2,400 checks, and 123 in 2020-21, split into 34 goals awarded, 42 disallowed, 29 penalties awarded, and 22 penalties overturned, with an average delay of about 50 seconds per review; LaLiga reviews about 12.55 incidents a match. Source: https://global.espn.com/football/story/_/id/37587139 and https://www.espn.com/soccer/story/_/id/37501924 (research sub-agent).
- Elite match injury incidence is about 21 injuries per 1000 match hours in 2022-23, down from 27.5 in 2001 to 2008, which is roughly one match injury per team every two to three matches; hamstring injuries are 24 percent of all injuries and rising. Source: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12406901/ and https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9985757/ (research sub-agent).
- Injury risk rises late in each half, with fixture congestion (5 of 8 studies find more match injuries with 4 days or fewer of rest, and muscle injuries rise within 5 days of the previous match), and with age, at an odds ratio of about 1.1 a year and 1.4 a year for hamstring strains. Source: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC9758680/ and https://doi.org/10.1177/0363546503258912 (research sub-agent).
- Conditions are measured only in part: altitude moves the expected goal difference by about half a goal per 1000 metres of difference, heat cuts running distance sharply (about 26 percent at 43 degrees against 21 degrees, weakly sourced), artificial turf shifts the injury mix toward ankle sprains (4.83 against 2.66 per 1000 match hours) without raising elite injury totals, and no source quantifies rain, wind, or pitch wear. Source: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18156225/ and https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC10139885/ (research sub-agent).

### The realism test: bands, design targets, watched reviews

- The realism test the engine plan already holds is three aggregate bands: goals per match from 2.4 to 3.2, shots per team from 8 to 16, and possession from 35 to 65 percent. Source: .ai/workflows/football-manager-match-engine/03-slice-calibration.md:33.
- Across 1826 matches in five top leagues in 2017/18, goals per match average 2.709 with a standard deviation of 1.675, and the distribution of total goals is Poisson to within 1.5 percentage points at every count, with a variance-to-mean ratio of 1.035. Source: .scratch/out/wyscout_rates.json (1826 matches, five leagues 2017/18).
